# Screen audit

This is the derive-lane (#2) audit of the 21 BMS maps the replacement ports to React screens. For every `DFHMDF` macro of every map it records the declared position, length and attributes, and what the screen shows or enforces for it; for every map it adds the header values the owning program moves to rows 1 and 2, and one row per function key the map labels or the program serves. It is the visual and validation source for `modern/frontend/src/screens/*.tsx`, the zod schemas in `modern/frontend/src/schemas/*.ts` and the field lengths of the request DTOs in `modern/crates/api/src/dto/*.rs`. Extracting the attributes is mechanical (#1) work, so every odd attribute or contradiction between sources is escalated below (R7), never smoothed over. Code that consumes this file is accepted only once the reviewer marks it `Accepted` in the status table of `modern/audit/README.md` (a9).

## Sources

- Maps: `app/bms/*.bms` (17), `app/app-authorization-ims-db2-mq/bms/{COPAU00,COPAU01}.bms` and `app/app-transaction-type-db2/bms/{COTRTLI,COTRTUP}.bms`, read in place.
- Symbolic maps: `app/cpy-bms/*.CPY`, `app/app-authorization-ims-db2-mq/cpy-bms/*.cpy` and `app/app-transaction-type-db2/cpy-bms/*.cpy`. Every named field of every map has its `I` and `O` fields there, with a length equal to the map's `LENGTH`; the cross-check found no mismatch.
- Header values: `app/cpy/COTTL01Y.cpy:18-22` (titles), `app/cpy/CSDAT01Y.cpy:30-41` (date and time layouts) and each program's header paragraph. Messages: `app/cpy/CSMSG01Y.cpy:18-21`. Menu options: `app/cpy/COMEN02Y.cpy:21` (11) and `app/cpy/COADM02Y.cpy:22` (6).
- Key handling: the AID `EVALUATE` of each owning program, in `app/cbl/`, `app/app-authorization-ims-db2-mq/cbl/` and `app/app-transaction-type-db2/cbl/`; transactions, maps and programs from `README.md:269-294`.
- BMS input mapping: IBM CICS TS 6.x, macro `DFHMDF`, operand `JUSTIFY` (https://www.ibm.com/docs/en/cics-ts/6.x?topic=macros-dfhmdf): `RIGHT` coded without `BLANK` or `ZERO` assumes `ZERO`; and topic "Formatted screen input" (https://www.ibm.com/docs/en/cics-ts/6.x?topic=data-formatted-screen-input): a field received with length zero is delivered as nulls, unpadded.

Source lines use short names: `<MAP>.bms`, `<MAP>.CPY`/`.cpy` and `<PROGRAM>.cbl` are in the folders above; `COTTL01Y.cpy`, `CSDAT01Y.cpy`, `CSMSG01Y.cpy`, `CSUTLDPY.cpy`, `COMEN02Y.cpy` and `COADM02Y.cpy` are in `app/cpy/`; `CIPAUDTY.cpy` is in `app/app-authorization-ims-db2-mq/cpy/`. A macro's lines run from its label line to its last continuation line (column 72 holds the continuation mark, and continued text resumes in column 16).

## Screens

The request counts 19 screens, the 17 maps of `app/bms` and the 2 of `app/app-authorization-ims-db2-mq/bms`. The repository holds 21: `COTRTLI` and `COTRTUP` in `app/app-transaction-type-db2/bms` serve the README rows CTLI and CTTU. All 21 are built; NAV reports the 19 as one group and these 2 as a second group (E21).

## How the rows are proved

Each test that proves a row carries `// audit: <Rule ID>`. The `Layer` column decides which tests those are (AAP 0.8.2):

- `both`: an input field's `LENGTH` and `NUM`, which the terminal enforced, the zod schema repeats and the request DTO revalidates. The server half is proved in `modern/crates/api/tests/endpoints_*.rs` (status, message and field of the rejection); the client half in `modern/frontend/src/schemas/<MAP>.test.ts`.
- `client`: labels, literal text, output-field placement, colours and emphasis, dark and hidden attributes, the header rows, the PF-key labels and bindings, and the keys that send no request (PF3, PF12, the clearing PF4, CTLI's PF2, the navigating selections). Proved by the render test `modern/frontend/src/screens/<MAP>.test.tsx`, by `modern/frontend/src/lib/pfkeys.test.ts`, or, for transfers, by `modern/frontend/e2e/nav.spec.ts`.
- `server`: not used. Every outcome a key produces through an endpoint is audited in `modern/audit/online/<PROGRAM>.md`, under the program paragraph each PF-key row names; the PF-key rows here prove only the label, the binding and the destination.

An input that no request carries is `client`, not `both`: the menu `OPTION` field and the selectors of `COCRDLI`, `COTRN00`, `COUSR00` and `COPAU00`, whose values only choose a route (AAP 0.3.4.1). No request DTO revalidates them, so their rows have no server half to prove.

## Reading the table

- Rule ID: `<MAP>-<nnn>`, the map member name; the macro rows are numbered in macro order from `001`, then the header rows, then the PF-key rows in key-row order, then the keys the program serves but the map does not label. Map names never collide with program names.
- Program: the program that sends and receives the map. Paragraph: the map name for a macro row; the program paragraph that moves the value for a header row; the paragraph that serves the key (from the AID `EVALUATE`) for a PF-key row.
- Copybook field or PIC: the field name and its symbolic-map `I` and `O` fields with their PICs; `(literal)` for an unnamed field with an `INITIAL`, `(unnamed; no symbolic field)` for one without.
- Input condition: for a macro row, the macro's operands exactly as declared (`POS`, `LENGTH`, `ATTRB`, `COLOR`, `HILIGHT`, then `JUSTIFY`, `PICIN`, `PICOUT`, `VALIDN` and `INITIAL` where present). An `INITIAL` keeps its source form, so `''` stands for one quote and `&&` for one ampersand. An omitted `ATTRB` means the BMS default `(ASKIP,NORM)`. For a header row, the send that moves the value; for a PF-key row, the key or the button click.
- Expected result: what the screen shows or enforces and the shadcn/ui component of AAP 0.4.2 that renders it.
- Placement, for every macro row: `FieldGrid` puts a field whose `POS` is `(r,c)` on grid row `r` with its attribute byte at column `c` and its data in columns `c+1` to `c+LENGTH`, using the `row-start-*`, `col-start-*` and `col-span-*` utilities of the 24 × 80 grid. `COLOR`, `BRT` and `HILIGHT=UNDERLINE` map to the theme tokens of AAP 0.4.3, which this file does not repeat.
- Attributes the program changes at run time (protection, colour, cursor, the brightening of dark key labels) are audited in `online/<PROGRAM>.md`. A row here names them only where the declared attribute differs from the field's role on the screen.

## Rules governing this file

- Single Component System: every expected result names one of the seven generated shadcn/ui components (`Button`, `Input`, `Label`, `Form`, `Table`, `Card`, `Alert`) or their parts, or Tailwind layout utilities for placement; no other component library appears.
- Cardholder Data Redaction: output fields that carry a card number (the `COCRDLI` rows, `COCRDUP`, `COTRN01`, the `COTRN02` key the program fills, `COPAU01`) show twelve `*` and the last four digits; the SSN on `COACTVW` and `COACTUP` shows `***-**-` and the last four digits. `COCRDSL` alone shows all 16 digits, and only for an `A` token (endpoint 8, AAP 0.3.4.8). `COACTUP`'s SSN parts are sent only when re-entered (E32), and dark password inputs are never pre-filled (E27). This file quotes no card number, SSN or password.
- Coverage Floors: each map's rows are what its render test `screens/<MAP>.test.tsx` tags, so every map keeps a row for every field and every key.
- Typed Dates and Timestamps: the screen date inputs (the `COACTUP` date parts, the `CORPT00` start and end date parts, the `COTRN02` original and processing dates) stay raw text that the program's date edit turns into typed dates, the exception AAP 0.3.4.1 makes; their rows say so. Displayed values of the `X(10)` date fields and `X(26)` timestamps AAP 0.3.3.1 enumerates (`ACCT-OPEN-DATE`, `ACCT-EXPIRAION-DATE`, `ACCT-REISSUE-DATE`, `CARD-EXPIRAION-DATE`, `CUST-DOB-YYYY-MM-DD`, `TRAN-ORIG-TS` and `TRAN-PROC-TS`, with their `CVTRA06Y` twins) come typed from the API. Any other date-like display field, such as the authorization date and time `COPAUS0C` and `COPAUS1C` format from `X(06)` text, travels as the program's display string; a display field's `LENGTH` never makes it a date.
- R7 — Anomaly handling: anomalies are cited by escalation ID or proposed below as `X-SCREENS-<n>`. R16 — Authority and protected paths: `app/**` is read only.

## Escalation candidates

Proposed under R7 — Anomaly handling. The reviewer folds each candidate into `ESCALATIONS.md` under the next free ID, as its `## Later entries` section describes; until then the rows above cite the candidate names.

- `X-SCREENS-1`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COSGN00.bms:75-93`; `app/cbl/COSGN00C.cbl:198-204`.
  - Anomaly class: request-vs-repository.
  - Finding: the sign-on screen shows the CICS application ID and system ID, which COSGN00C obtains with `EXEC CICS ASSIGN APPLID` and `ASSIGN SYSID`. No CICS region exists in the replacement, and no endpoint serves the sign-on screen before ENTER.
  - Proposed resolution: the `AppID:` and `SysID:` labels and their read-only fields are rendered, and both fields stay empty (COSGN00-044, COSGN00-045).
- `X-SCREENS-2`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COSGN00.bms:175-180`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: `PASSWD` is declared `ATTRB=(DRK,FSET,UNPROT)` with `INITIAL='________'`. With `FSET` the terminal transmits the field even when untouched, so an operator who does not overtype all eight positions sends underscores as part of the password; the in-stream user records hold eight-character passwords, which overtype them all.
  - Proposed resolution: the password input starts empty, is never pre-filled and sends only what is typed; endpoint 1 pads it with spaces to `X(08)`, as an erased field would arrive.
- `X-SCREENS-3`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COSGN00.bms:161-169,181-189`; `app/bms/COCRDLI.bms:140-308`; `app/bms/COUSR01.bms:136-145`, `app/bms/COUSR02.bms:140-149`, `app/bms/COUSR03.bms:125-134`; `app/app-authorization-ims-db2-mq/bms/COPAU01.bms:199-208`; `app/app-authorization-ims-db2-mq/bms/COPAU00.bms:453-493`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: declarations overlap or leave position order. `COSGN00` declares a zero-length stopper and an `(8 Char)` literal at the same position (19,52), and again at (20,52). `COCRDLI` declares each `CRDSTPn` at the position of the zero-length stopper before it, rows 2 to 7, and row 1 has no `CRDSTP1`. The `User Type: ` literal of `COUSR01`–`COUSR03` (`LENGTH=11`) reaches the attribute byte of `USRTYPE`. `COPAU01` declares `Tran Id:` with `LENGTH=18`, through column 20, over `TRNID` at (15,12). `COPAU00` declares `SEL0005` after the other fields of its row.
  - Proposed resolution: every field is placed at its declared position; where two declarations claim a byte, the later field's attribute byte wins, as the terminal shows it, so a literal's trailing padding yields to the next field and no text is lost. `SEL0005` renders in the selector column of row 5.
- `X-SCREENS-4`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COACTVW.bms:363-364`; `app/bms/COACTUP.bms:487-488`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: an unnamed `DFHMDF LENGTH=9,POS=(1,1)` with no `ATTRB` and no `INITIAL`, declared after `INFOMSG` and out of position order, lies over the `Tran:` literal at (1,1). It has no symbolic field, so no program writes it.
  - Proposed resolution: not rendered; the `Tran:` label and `TRNNAME` keep their declared places.
- `X-SCREENS-5`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COACTVW.bms:84-90`; `app/cpy-bms/COACTVW.CPY:60`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: only this account-number field carries `PICIN='99999999999'` and `VALIDN=(MUSTFILL)`. On a terminal with field validation the operator cannot leave or transmit the field partly filled, so a short account number never reaches COACTVWC's `2210-EDIT-ACCOUNT`. No other field of the 21 maps declares `PICIN` or `VALIDN`.
  - Proposed resolution: the zod schema rejects a non-empty value shorter than 11 characters, as the terminal did, while the server keeps COACTVWC's audited edit for any value it receives.
- `X-SCREENS-6`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COSGN00.bms:190-193`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: an unnamed one-byte dark unprotected field at (20,61) has no symbolic field, so COSGN00C never reads it.
  - Proposed resolution: not rendered.
- `X-SCREENS-7`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COCRDSL.bms:144-152`; `app/bms/COCRDUP.bms:154-162`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: `ERRMSG` is declared `LENGTH=80` at (23,1). Its data runs through row 24, column 1, the attribute byte of `FKEYS`; the other 19 maps declare 78.
  - Proposed resolution: `MessageLine` shows up to 80 characters on row 23 and the key row keeps its own row.
- `X-SCREENS-8`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COUSR01.bms:155-159`; `app/cbl/COUSR01C.cbl:90-103`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: the key row labels `F12=Exit`, but COUSR01C's AID `EVALUATE` has no PF12 branch, so PF12 takes `WHEN OTHER` and shows `Invalid key pressed. Please see below...`.
  - Proposed resolution: the label is rendered as a `Button`, and pressing it shows the invalid-key message, as the COBOL does (COUSR01-038).
- `X-SCREENS-9`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COUSR02.bms:159-164`; `app/cbl/COUSR02C.cbl:111-119`.
  - Anomaly class: request-vs-repository.
  - Finding: the key row labels `F3=Save&Exit`, and COUSR02C performs `UPDATE-USER-INFO` before it transfers to the admin menu. AAP 0.3.5 lists F3 on COUSR02 as back, and AAP 0.3.4.1 lists only PF5 for endpoint 19.
  - Proposed resolution: F3 sends endpoint 19 exactly as F5, then navigates back whatever the outcome, which keeps the COBOL's observable behaviour (COUSR02-037). The update itself is audited in `online/COUSR02C.md`.
- `X-SCREENS-10`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COACTVW.bms:369-373`, `app/cbl/COACTVWC.cbl:306-314`; `app/bms/COCRDLI.bms:335-339`, `app/cbl/COCRDLIC.cbl:370-380`; `app/app-authorization-ims-db2-mq/bms/COPAU01.bms:288-292`, `app/app-authorization-ims-db2-mq/cbl/COPAUS1C.cbl:181-183`; `app/app-transaction-type-db2/bms/COTRTLI.bms:312-336`, `app/app-transaction-type-db2/cbl/COTRTLIC.cbl:574-587`; `app/bms/COUSR03.bms:144-148`, `app/cbl/COUSR03C.cbl:123-125`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: five programs serve a key their map does not label: ENTER on `COACTVW`, `COCRDLI`, `COPAU01` and `COTRTLI`, and PF12 on `COUSR03`.
  - Proposed resolution: each such key gets a `Button` labelled with the bare key name (`ENTER`, `F12`), because AAP 0.4.4 requires every PF action to exist as a `Button`; no action text is invented, and the Enter key also submits the form.
- `X-SCREENS-11`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COSGN00.bms:57-60,70-74`; `app/cpy-bms/COSGN00.CPY:54`; `app/cpy/CSDAT01Y.cpy:36-41`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: on `COSGN00` alone `CURTIME` is `LENGTH=9` with `INITIAL='Ahh:mm:ss'`, and `PGMNAME` and `CURTIME` are `PROT` rather than `ASKIP`. COSGN00C moves the 8-byte `WS-CURTIME-HH-MM-SS` into the 9-byte field.
  - Proposed resolution: the time shows as `HH:MM:SS` followed by one blank position, as on every other map; the protection difference has no visible effect on output fields.
- `X-SCREENS-12`
  - Found in: `app/app-transaction-type-db2/bms/COTRTUP.bms:126-130`; `app/app-transaction-type-db2/cbl/COTRTUPC.cbl:577-621,1397-1423`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: the dark label `FKEY06` (`F6=Add`) is never brightened, and `0001-CHECK-PFKEYS` never accepts PF6.
  - Proposed resolution: no `F6` button and no binding; the label is never visible on the 3270 either.
- `X-SCREENS-13`
  - Found in: `app/app-transaction-type-db2/bms/COTRTLI.bms:280-300`; `app/app-transaction-type-db2/cbl/COTRTLIC.cbl:60,434-457`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: the map declares an eighth list row (`TRTSELA`, `TRTTYPA`, `TRTDSCA` on row 19), but COTRTLIC's row array `EACH-ROWI OCCURS 7 TIMES` and `WS-MAX-SCREEN-LINES` 7 stop before it, so the row is never written and always blank.
  - Proposed resolution: the list `Table` renders seven rows (AAP 0.3.5); the eighth row is not rendered.
- `X-SCREENS-14`
  - Found in: `app/bms/COCRDLI.bms:140-308`, `app/cbl/COCRDLIC.cbl:748-835`; `app/bms/COCRDUP.bms:84-88`, `app/cbl/COCRDUPC.cbl:1168-1205`; `app/app-transaction-type-db2/bms/COTRTLI.bms:133-277`, `app/app-transaction-type-db2/cbl/COTRTLIC.cbl:1329-1374`.
  - Anomaly class: declaration-not-followed.
  - Finding: fields the operator enters are declared protected, and fields declared unprotected are protected by the program: the `COCRDLI` selectors (`PROT`, made enterable on rows that hold a card), `COCRDUP` `ACCTSID` (`PROT`, enterable until details are fetched), the `COTRTLI` selectors (`PROT`, enterable on rows that hold a type) and descriptions (`UNPROT`, protected except on the row of a single valid `U`).
  - Proposed resolution: each field takes its run-time role: the rows above describe the selectors and `ACCTSID` as inputs, and the per-state protection is audited in the online files of COCRDLIC, COCRDUPC and COTRTLIC.
- `X-SCREENS-15`
  - Found in: `app/cbl/COACTUPC.cbl:2572-2583`; `app/cbl/COCRDUPC.cbl:484-497,958-962`; `app/app-transaction-type-db2/cbl/COTRTUPC.cbl:94-97,352-354,405-419,469-478,524-533,988-1010`.
  - Anomaly class: request-vs-repository.
  - Finding: on CAUP and CCUP, PF12 re-reads the record (`9000-READ-ACCT`, `9000-READ-DATA`), so a change another user committed since the fetch appears after cancel. AAP 0.3.4.1 makes PF12 client-side, calling no endpoint. CTTU reads nothing on PF12. PF12 never performs `1000-PROCESS-INPUTS`, whose edits alone can make `FLG-TRANFILTER-ISVALID` (low-values, lines 94-97) true (lines 706, 741, 832), so `WS-EDIT-TTYP-FLAG` keeps the spaces of `INITIALIZE WS-MISC-STORAGE` (lines 352-354), the condition is false, and the `9000-READ-TRANTYPE` of line 991 never runs. The outcome depends on the state. In states S (details shown), X (details not found) and R (create new record), lines 405-419 simulate initial entry and lines 469-478 send the fresh blank screen with an empty error row. In states N (changes OK, not confirmed) and 9 (delete confirmation), lines 524-533 perform `2000-DECIDE-ACTION`, whose PF12 branch (lines 988-1010) takes the `ELSE`: state 9 shows `Delete was cancelled` on the fresh blank screen, the details not restored; state N shows `Update was cancelled` and moves to state B (changes backed out), which shows the fetched code and description again (lines 1153-1156), or, when the old values are blank (a new type), a blank screen, after which the next key other than F3 shows the fresh screen (lines 412-414).
  - Proposed resolution: CAUP and CCUP: PF12 discards the edits and shows the values of the screen's last fetch response again; a change made by another user since then shows at the next fetch. The reviewer decides whether PF12 should instead repeat endpoint 5 or 9. CTTU: the screen reproduces the state-specific outcomes client-side with no request, which matches the COBOL because it reads nothing (`online/COTRTUPC.md` COTRTUPC-039 to COTRTUPC-041; X-COTRTUPC-8).
